--- Model Response ---
To determine the minimum number of nodes that must be removed to completely disconnect the two black nodes (J and E), I will analyze the paths between them and identify the critical nodes whose removal will sever all paths.

### Step-by-Step Reasoning:

1. **Identify the Target Nodes:**
   - The two black nodes are J and E.

2. **Trace All Paths Between J and E:**
   - Path 1: J -> P -> I -> C -> N -> B -> H -> G -> K -> F -> E
   - Path 2: J -> P -> O -> Y -> N -> B -> H -> G -> K -> F -> E
   - Path 3: J -> P -> I -> C -> N -> B -> H -> F -> E
   - Path 4: J -> P -> O -> Y -> N -> B -> H -> F -> E
   - Path 5: J -> M -> L -> E

3. **Identify Critical Nodes:**
   - To disconnect J and E, we need to remove nodes that are common to all paths.
   - Node F is a common node in Paths 1, 2, 3, and 4.
   - Node B is a common node in Paths 1, 2, 3, and 4.
   - Node H is a common node in Paths 1, 2, 3, and 4.
   - Node N is a common node in Paths 1, 2, 3, and 4.
   - Node M is a critical node in Path 5.

4. **Verify the Minimum Cut Set:**
   - Removing node F will break Paths 1, 2, 3, and 4.
   - Removing node M will break Path 5.
   - Therefore, removing nodes F and M will disconnect J and E.

### Final Answer:

Nodes to remove: 2, Cut Set: [F, M]
--- Ground Truth ---

### Step-by-Step Reasoning:

To disconnect the two black nodes \( I \) and \( E \), I need to remove the minimum number of nodes such that no path exists between \( I \) and \( E \). Here's the analysis of the connections:

1. **Direct Connections to Black Nodes:**
   - Node \( I \) is directly connected to nodes \( B \), \( H \), \( P \), and \( J \).
   - Node \( E \) is directly connected to nodes \( B \), \( P \), \( L \), \( M \), and \( J \).

2. **Identifying Intermediate Nodes:**
   - **Node \( B \)**: Connected to \( I \), \( C \), \( O \), and \( E \).
   - **Node \( P \)**: Connected to \( I \), \( H \), and \( E \).
   - **Node \( J \)**: Connected to \( I \), \( F \), \( D \), and \( E \).
   - **Node \( H \)**: Connected to \( I \), \( C \), \( G \), and \( P \).
   - **Node \( C \)**: Connected to \( O \), \( B \), \( N \), and \( H \).
   - **Node \( F \)**: Connected to \( K \), \( X \), \( G \), and \( J \).
   - **Node \( G \)**: Connected to \( K \), \( X \), \( H \), and \( F \).
   - **Node \( K \)**: Connected to \( X \), \( G \), and \( Y \).
   - **Node \( X \)**: Connected to \( K \), \( G \), and \( F \).
   - **Node \( Y \)**: Connected to \( N \) and \( K \).
   - **Node \( N \)**: Connected to \( C \), \( O \), and \( Y \).
   - **Node \( O \)**: Connected to \( C \) and \( B \).
   - **Node \( L \)**: Directly connected to \( E \) and \( M \).
   - **Node \( M \)**: Directly connected to \( E \) and \( L \).
   - **Node \( D \)**: Directly connected to \( J \).

3. **Paths Analysis:**
   - Paths from \( I \) to \( E \) can be reached through nodes \( B \), \( P \), and \( J \).
   - If we remove nodes \( B \), \( P \), and \( J \), any path from \( I \) to \( E \) would be disrupted as there would be no intermediate nodes left connecting these two black nodes.

4. **Verifying the Minimality:**
   - Removing just one of \( B \), \( P \), or \( J \) would still leave paths intact. For example, removing just \( B \) would leave paths through \( P \) and \( J \).
   - Removing two out of \( B \), \( P \), and \( J \) would leave connections through the third node.
   - Thus, all three nodes \( B \), \( P \), and \( J \) are necessary to ensure complete disconnection.

### Final Answer:

Nodes to remove: 3, Cut Set: [B, P, J]

2. **Trace Paths Between E and J**:
   - Path 1: E -> B -> P -> J
   - Path 2: E -> B -> C -> I -> H -> G -> F -> X -> K -> H -> P -> J
   - Path 3: E -> B -> P -> H -> I -> C -> O -> P -> J
   - Path 4: E -> L -> M -> D -> J
   - Path 5: E -> B -> O -> Y -> N -> C -> I -> H -> G -> K -> X -> F -> G -> H -> P -> J (a long and winding path but possible)

3. **Analyze Critical Nodes**:
   - Node `B`: Connects E to multiple nodes (C, P, O, L) and is a crucial junction.
   - Node `P`: Acts as a bridge between many paths involving E and J.
   - Node `H`: Another critical junction connecting multiple paths involving E and J.
   - Node `I`: Connects through H and P to create paths between E and J.
   - Node `C`: Connects through B and I to create paths between E and J.
   - Node `G`, `F`, `X`, `K`: Part of long paths but can be bypassed through other nodes.

4. **Determine Minimum Cut Set**:
   - Removing `B` and `P` will break all paths between E and J.
     - Removing `B` breaks Paths 1, 2, 3, and 5.
     - Removing `P` breaks Paths 1, 2, 3, and 4.

### Verification:
- After removing `B` and `P`, all possible paths between E and J are severed.
- No smaller set of nodes can achieve the same disconnection.

--- Model Response ---
Step-by-step reasoning:

1. The target nodes are G and N.
2. To disconnect G and N, I need to identify all possible paths between them and ensure that no path remains after removing a minimal set of edges.

**Paths from G to N:**
- G-H-B-C-N
- G-H-I-C-N
- G-H-B-I-C-N
- G-H-F-E-I-C-N
- G-H-F-E-M-J-P-D-J-I-C-N

3. To disconnect G and N, I need to remove key edges that eliminate all these paths.

**Key Edges to Remove:**
- Removing (G, H) or (H, B) will disallow paths to turn below H.
- Removing (H, F) ensures that the path cannot use F to get to N via multiple paths through E and I.
- Removing (I, C) ensures that no path from I can go to N.

By considering all paths and key edges critical.
Let's select the following minimal cut:

- Remove (G, H): This directly blocks paths from G to H and thus to N.
- Remove (H, I): This blocks the path through H and F to I, hence indirectly blocks paths through I going to N.
- Remove (I, C): This ensures no path exists through I to go directly or indirectly to N.

This set of 3 edges completely disconnects G and N.

Final Answer: Edges to remove: 3, Cut Set: [(G, H), (H, I), (I, C)]

--- Model Response ---
Step-by-step reasoning. The target nodes are H and N. Here are the steps to determine the minimum number of edges to remove to disconnect H and N:

1. **Identify the immediate neighbors of each target node:**
   - Node H is directly connected to nodes B, E, and I.
   - Node N is directly connected to nodes B, C, and O.

2. **Trace possible paths from H to N:**
   - Path 1: H -> B -> N
   - Path 2: H -> B -> C -> N
   - Path 3: H -> I -> P -> O -> N
   - Path 4: H -> E -> M -> L -> J -> P -> O -> N
   - Path 5: H -> B -> C -> O -> N

3. **Determine where to cut these paths to ensure disconnection:**
   - To disconnect Path 1: Remove (B, H) or (B, N)
   - To disconnect Path 2: Remove (B, C) (already disconnects Path 1 as well)
   - To disconnect Path 3: Remove (I, P) or (P, O)
   - To disconnect Path 4: Remove (M, L) or (L, J) or (P, O)  (or (E, M), but more efficient to pair above)
   - To disconnect Path 5: The removal of (B, C) already grabs this path

  To minimize the cuts, consider disconnection in the middle of the densest paths:

   - (B, H) breaks Path 1.
   - (I, P) breaks Path 3
   - (P, O) or (E, M), effectively blocks Path 4.

Finally, the set of edges [(B, H), (I, P), (E, M)] removes three edges from the graph and is a valid minimal cut.

Final Answer: Edges to remove: 3, Cut Set: [(B, H), (I, P), (E, M)]

   - The target nodes are F and D.

   - **Starting from F:**
     - F -> E -> B -> C -> O -> P -> D
     - F -> E -> B -> C -> N -> Y -> D
     - F -> E -> H -> I -> P -> D
     - F -> E -> H -> I -> O -> C -> P -> D
     - F -> E -> H -> I -> O -> P -> D
     - F -> X -> K -> G -> H -> I -> P -> D
     - F -> X -> K -> G -> H -> I -> O -> P -> D

3. **Identify Critical Edges:**
   - To disconnect F from D, we need to remove edges that are common in all paths.
   - The edge (P, D) is a critical edge since it appears in multiple paths.
   - Next, we need to ensure that there are no alternative paths. We observe that:
     - Removing (P, D) eliminates direct connections to D from the middle section of the graph.
     - To fully disconnect D from paths through F, we need to remove another critical edge that connects back to the central part of the graph.
     - The edge (E, H) is another critical edge because it connects to H, which leads to multiple paths.

4. **Verify Minimal Cut Set:**
   - By removing (P, D) and (E, H), we ensure that all paths from F to D are severed.
   - No smaller set of edges can disconnect F and D completely.

### Final Answer:

Edges to remove: 2, Cut Set: [(E, H), (P, D)]

   - Check direct connections to J: None.

   - **Neighbors of E:** L, M, H, B
   - **Neighbors of H:** F, B, E, C
   - Check direct connections to J: None.

   - **Neighbors of M:** L, E, I
   - **Neighbors of B:** H, E, N, C
   - **Neighbors of C:** B, N, O, P
   - Check direct connections to J: None.

4. **Level 4 (Neighbors of I, N, O, P):**
   - **Neighbors of I:** M, J, C, P
   - **Neighbors of N:** B, C, Y
   - **Neighbors of O:** C, Y, P
   - **Neighbors of P:** I, O, J, D
   - Check direct connections to J: I to J, P to J.

From the above search, we have found two paths of the same minimal length:
   - Path 1: F → E → I → J
   - Path 2: F → E → M → I → J

Since both paths have the same length, we can choose either one. The shortest path length is 3.

**Final Answer:** Length: 3, Path: [F, E, I, J].

1. Directly from L to E then to B, then to N, then to C, then to H, then to G, then to F, then to K, then to X, then to M, then to I, then to P, then to J. This is an example of levels of connection but there are many routes between these nodes due to redundant connections. Picking the simplified path options:

Possible simplified paths:
- Path 1: L → E → B → N → C → H → G → F → K → X → M → I → P → J
- Path 2: L → E → G → H → C → O → D → P → J
- Path 3: L → E → G → H → C → B → N → Y → H → I → P → J

### Critical Cutting Nodes
Given the complexity and the cross-connections in the graph, several edges need to be cut to ensure complete disconnection of L and J. The minimal cut-set is essential, so we must examine multiple points where all potential flows could be broken.

We need to consider critical edges that act as bridges in multiple paths:
- Edge (E, G): This connects L’s direct neighborhood to J’s network; removing it cuts one path.
- Edge (C, H): Another crucial link to both L and J.
- Edge (P, I): Direct neighbor to J which further limits remaining alternate paths.

Based on the paths identified, we see removing exactly three edges that disconnect these significant links/neighborhoods ensures no possible path remains between L and J. Removing fewer than three edges in this setup might still hold alternative routes due to parallel paths.

### Minimal Cut Set
Removing (E, G), (C, H), and (P, I) will guarantee no route from L to J remains active, based on simplified traverse examination and critical points:

Final Answer:
Edges to remove: 3, Cut Set: [(E, G), (C, H), (P, I)]

     - H → G → F → E → I → J → P → O → N
     - H → B → C → O → Y → N

   - The paths from H to N must be broken by removing the minimum number of edges.
   - To ensure disconnection, we need to remove one edge from each of these paths such that no alternative path exists.

4. **Select Minimum Edges to Remove**:
   - **Edge (C, O)**: This is a direct connection in one of the paths (H → B → C → O → N).
   - **Edge (B, C)**: This breaks the path (H → B → C → O → N) and also impacts other paths considering different routes.
   - **Edge (J, P)**: This breaks the second path (H → G → F → E → I → J → P → O → N).

   - Removing the edges (C, O), (B, C), and (J, P) ensures that there are no remaining paths from H to N.
   - No smaller set of edges can achieve this disconnection without leaving at least one path intact.

   - J → D → M → L → E
   - J → P → O → Y → N → B → F → E

   - J → P → O → C → I → B → H → G → K → X → F → E

     - J → P must be disconnected.
     - P → O must be disconnected.
     - N → B must be disconnected.
     - B → F must be disconnected.

4. **Validate the Cut Set:**
   - Removing edges (J, P), (P, O), and (B, F) will disconnect all paths between J and E:
     - Removing (J, P) disconnects the primary path through P.
     - Removing (P, O) ensures no alternative routes through P to O.
     - Removing (B, F) ensures no routes through B to F.
   - We need to remove edges (J, P), (P, O), and (B, F).

5. **Final Cut Set:**
   - The minimum cut set includes [(J, P), (P, O), (B, F)].
   - To further optimize, recognize alternate ways to cut:
     - Instead of removing (B, F), consider (P, O) and (M, L) with (F, X) which ensures minimal removal through shared junction of F and X.

6. **Reconsideration for Smaller Cut:**
   - Removing (J, P) and critical paths optionally only necessitate disconnect at (B, F) within all multi-structure relinks ensuring that no path persists.
   - A more fit removal is minimal effective even for redundancy where (B, F) and (J, P) a collective but optimized to any alternate conformations.

### Final Answer:

Image name: PGS_task3_gt2_PAIR2
Task: task3_min_edges_removal
Model: mistral-small-3.1-24b

--- Model Response ---
### Answer:

**Step-by-step reasoning:**

The target nodes are G and I. To disconnect these nodes, we need to identify all possible paths between them and determine the minimum number of edges to remove to ensure no path exists between G and I. Here are the steps:

1. **Identify paths between G and I:**
   - G-H-B-N-Y-O-C-I
   - G-H-B-N-O-I
   - G-F-L-E-K-J-D-P-C-I
   - G-H-B-N-Y-O-P-C-I

2. **Evaluate critical edges to remove:**
   - **Remove edge (H, B):** This cuts the paths going through B.
   - **Remove edge (F, L):** This cuts the path going through L.
   - **Remove edge (C, I):** This cuts all paths involving C.
   - **Remove edge (N, Y):** This avoids any bypasses involving Y.
   - **Remove edge (N, O):** This ensures no path connects via O.
   - **Remove edge (O, P):** Ensures no path is left to P.

3. **Construct the minimal cut set:**
   - Removing edges (H, B) and (C, I) ensures that all possible paths between G and I are severed. Removing one of these edges alone would leave alternative paths still viable. Therefore, these two edges form the minimal cut set needed to disconnect G and I without removing the target nodes.

**Final Answer:**
Edges to remove: 2, Cut Set: [(H, B), (C, I)]
--- Ground Truth ---
{'length': 2, 'cut_set': [['F', 'E'], ['G', 'H']]}
